    O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. The High Court of Madhya Pradesh at Jabalpur, by the
impugned judgment and order dated 08 th September, 2026,
has dismissed the appellant’s application for grant of bail. 
3. Appellant,  figuring  as  an  accused  in  FIR
No.RC0082025A0006  dated  09 th May,  2025  registered  at
Police  Station  CBI/SPE,  Bhopal,  District  Bhopal,  Madhya
Pradesh  under Sections 13(2) r/w 13(1)(a) of Prevention of
Corruption Act, 1988 (amended in 2018) and Section 61(2)
Crl. Appeal @ SLP (Crl.) No.17855/2026  1

--- Page 14 ---
r/w 318(4) of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023, (Section
120-B r/w 420 of Indian Penal Code, 1860), was arrested on
08th June, 2026.
4. We  have  heard learned counsel for the  parties and
perused  the  counter  affidavit  of  the  respondent- Central
Bureau of Investigation (CBI).
5. Charge-sheet  under  Sections  61(2),  318(4)  of  the
Bharatiya  Nyaya  Sanhita,  2023,  r/w  13(1)(a)  of  the
Prevention  of  Corruption  Act,  1988  was  filed  on  01 st
November, 2025. In all, there are 68 accused.
6. However,  the  charges  have  not  been  framed.
Prosecution is still at the stage of securing the presence of
the accused. Thus, it is clear that the trial is unlikely to
conclude soon.
7. T aking  an  overall  view  of  the  matter,  we  find  that
further detention of the appellant in custody may not serve
any useful purpose; hence, we are inclined to accept the
appeal and grant relief as prayed by admitting the appellant
to an order for grant of bail.
Crl. Appeal @ SLP (Crl.) No.17855/2026  2

--- Page 15 ---
8. Accordingly, we set aside the impugned judgment and
order.
9. Appellant  shall  be  released  on  bail,  subject  to
furnishing of bail bonds to the satisfaction of the trial court
and subject to such other terms and conditions as may be
imposed by it.
10. Needless to observe, if released on bail, the appellant
shall  not,  directly  or  indirectly,  by  making  inducement,
threat or promise, dissuade any person acquainted with the
facts of the case from disclosing such facts to the court.
11. In  the  event there  is  any breach of the  terms  and
conditions for grant of bail, the trial court shall be at liberty
to cancel the bail of the appellant.
12. It  is  also  ordered  that  the  appellant  shall  diligently
attend  proceedings  of  the  trial,  unless  exempted.  If  he
abstains from attending the proceedings without justifiable
cause, that could also be seen as breach of the conditions
for  grant  of  bail  and  the  trial  court  will  be  free  to  pass
appropriate orders.
Crl. Appeal @ SLP (Crl.) No.17855/2026  3

--- Page 16 ---
13. We clarify that the observations made in this order and
grant of bail will not be treated as findings on the merits of
the case.
14. The appeal is, accordingly, allowed on the aforesaid
terms.
15. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed of.

CITY MONTESSORI SCHOOL                           Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
STATE OF U.P & ORS.                              Respondent(s)
IA No. 322473/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING C/C OF THE IMPUGNED
JUDGMENT,IA No. 322474/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.
IA No. 322472/2026 - PERMISSION TO FILE LENGTHY LIST OF DATES
 
Date : 27-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.

         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE J.B. PARDIWALA

For Petitioner(s) : Mr. Kapil Sibal, Sr. Adv.

1

--- Page 20 ---
          SLP(C) NO.37043 OF 2026
                   Mr. G.p. Singh Chauhan, Adv.
                   Mr. R. P. Gupta- 597, AOR
                   
                   
For Respondent(s) : Ms. Malvik Trivedi, Sr. Adv.
                   Mr. Ankit Yadav- 2925, AOR
                   Mr. Shailendra Slaria, Adv.
                   Ms. Sujal Gupta, Adv.
                   Ms. Shivangi Gulati, Adv.
                   Ms. Aastha Harshwal, Adv.
                   Mr. Raman Singh Chauhan, Adv.
                   
         Mr. Mukesh Verma, Adv.
Mr. Shashank Singh, AOr
UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The issue is in a very narrow compass as to the
participation of the petitioner in the proceedings before
the  Lucknow  Development  Authority  (for  short,  ‘the
Authority’) which has been initiated under Section 27 of
the U.P. Urban Planning and Development Act, 1973 (the Act
of 1973) which notice has been issued against the 5th
respondent  herein.  The  5th  respondent  challenged  the
proceedings before the High Court, in which an order was
passed, directing the writ petitioner to file a detailed
response to the notice with all relevant documents and any
report in their favour, upon which the Authority was given
2

--- Page 21 ---
          SLP(C) NO.37043 OF 2026
liberty to carry out inspection by an officer of the rank
not less than that of Chief Engineer/Additional Secretary
within four weeks thereafter. The High Court also directed
consideration of the same independently and impartially
without being influenced by any further action at the
behest of the CMS, the petitioner herein.
2. Mr. Kapil Sibal, learned Senior Counsel appeared for
the petitioner submitted that the petitioner cannot be
kept away from the hearing and the petitioner does not
have any objection with respect to the other portion of
the  judgment,  including  that  of  an  inspection  and
consideration to be made independently and impartially.
3. Ms. Malvik Trivedi, learned Senior Counsel appearing
for the respondent, however, submitted that there is a
history of the dispute between the two parties and the
petitioner has been constantly trying to take over the
property in the possession of the 5th respondent.  It is
also submitted that as of now, an order dated 21.10.2026
has been passed by the Authority in Suit No.2092 of 2025,
Online Case No.LDA/Z6/ANI/2025/0006284.
3

--- Page 22 ---
          SLP(C) NO.37043 OF 2026
4. On brief facts, it is to be noticed that a total area
of 23000 sq. ft. was owned by Smt. Urmila Bhalla and
Smt. Sheela Kapoor. Out of the total extent 6000 sq. ft.
was leased out by the 5th respondent’s predecessor, and
17000 sq. ft. was leased out by City Montessori School,
the petitioner herein. There were multiple proceedings
initiated  for  eviction  of  the  CMS  which  eventually
culminated in an acquisition being carried out for the
purposes of the CMS. However, when such acquisition was
proceeded with, the entire 23000 sq. ft. was acquired
which was successfully challenged in a Writ Petition.  The
challenge to this Court resulted in the acquisition being
upheld for 17000 sq. ft. relating to CMS, while setting it
aside insofar 6000 sq. ft.  While the matter was pending
before this Court, the 6000 sq. ft. was also conceded to
the lessee by the Government. The present proceedings were
initiated at the behest of the CMS, alleging unauthorized
constructions.  The  dispute  with  respect  to  the
unauthorized construction also went through various rounds
and eventually it has ended with the present notice under
4

--- Page 23 ---
          SLP(C) NO.37043 OF 2026
Section 27 issued under the Act of 1973.
5. We need not touch upon the merits of the dispute or
the allegation of unauthorized construction. Suffice it to
notice that the direction was only to consider the issue
independently and impartially, without being influenced by
any action carried out at the behest of CMS. This is not
to say that the CMS should not be heard, especially when
it is on their initiative, the complaint was filed.
Whether the allegation is valid or not is definitely left
to the independent and impartial consideration of the
Authority. But it could never have been intended that the
complainant is kept away from the proceedings. The order
passed on 21.10.2026 obviously is not after affording a
hearing to the CMS.
6. In such circumstances, we set aside the order dated
21.10.2026 only to facilitate fresh consideration.  The
Authority shall issue notice both to the CMS and the 5th
respondent and shall supply a copy of the reply and the
Annexures produced by the 5th respondent to the CMS.  If
any inspection report is on record, as indicated by the
5

--- Page 24 ---
          SLP(C) NO.37043 OF 2026
impugned judgment, then the same also shall be served on
the parties.  The notice shall indicate a date within one
month from today and on the said date or any other date to
which  the  hearing  is  adjourned,  with  acknowledgment
personally taken from the parties or their authorized
representatives who appear on the first day, the matter
shall be concluded. We make it clear that we have not
looked into the merits of the order passed by the High
Court on 01.10.2026 and have interfered with it only on
the grounds of violation of the principles of natural
justice. The matter shall be considered on its own merits,
in accordance with law.
7. The Special Leave Petition stands disposed of with
the above directions.
8. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand rejected.
(CHANDRESH)                        (POOJA SHARMA)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                COURT MASTER (NSH)

--- Page 25 ---

1. Leave granted.
2. Heard learned counsel for the parties.
3. This appeal, inter alia, seeks modification
of  the  bail  condition  to  the  extent  it
requires the bail applicant to submit two
local sureties.
4. It is the case of the appellant that other
conditions have already been fulfilled.  In
so far as the condition of submitting local
sureties is concerned, it has been fulfilled
Crl.A. @ SLP (Crl.) No.19796 of 2026                                                   1

--- Page 26 ---
to the extent of one local surety.  However,
he is not in a position to obtain other local
surety as a result of which he has not been
able to derive the fruits of the bail order.
Additionally, there is a condition to deposit
the passport.
5. It has been submitted that in respect of the
condition requiring deposit of passport, the
passport has already been deposited with CGST
Belapur of which proof has been annexed as
‘Annexure – P-9’.
6. Having  regard  to  the  above,  we  deem  it
appropriate to modify the bail conditions to
the extent indicated below:
(i)  In place of two local sureties one local
surety shall suffice and the other surety may
be  any  one  to  the  satisfaction  of  the
concerned Magistrate.
(ii)  Instead of depositing the passport, the
appellant shall furnish proof of deposit of
the passport along with an undertaking that
he will not seek release of the passport
where  it  is  already  deposited  without
permission of the Trial Court.
Crl.A. @ SLP (Crl.) No.19796 of 2026                                                   2

--- Page 27 ---
7. If the modified conditions, as directed above
are  fulfilled,  the  appellant  shall  be
released  pursuant  to  the  bail  order
concerned.

terms.

stand disposed of.

SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (CIVIL) NO.21124 OF 2023
KETAN PRAKASH GHOTGE & ANR.                 PETITIONER(S)
                                VERSUS
NUTAN PRAKASH GHOTGE                        RESPONDENT(S)
O R D E R
Being  aggrieved  by  the  order  dated
15.09.2023 passed in Writ Petition No.11684 of
2023 by the High Court of Judicature at Bombay,
the appellants are before this Court.
Learned counsel for the petitioners as well
as learned counsel for the respondent submitted in
unison that during the pendency of this Special
Leave Petition before this Court, the parties have
arrived at a settlement of their disputes on
25.05.2026 and hence, IA No.169161 of 2026 has
been filed as a joint application by the parties
seeking certain directions which read as under:
“a. Allow the present application and
dispose of the present petition in terms of
the  attached  Consent  Terms  dated
25.05.2026; and 
b. Request/direct the Hon’ble Bombay High
Court to expeditiously hear and dispose of
Testamentary  Petition  No.2444  of  2021
1

--- Page 31 ---
preferably within two months; and 
c. Pass any other order as it may deem
fit in the facts and circumstances of this
case and in the interest of justice.”
We  have  heard  learned  counsel  for  the
respective parties.
The parties have also appeared before this
Court through video conferencing facility. When
queried by the Court, they stated that they have
agreed to the consent terms on their own free will
and  volition  without  there  being  any  undue
influence or coercion on any side. The parties
have also stated that they would abide by the
consent terms and they requested this Court to
dispose of this Special Leave Petition with a
direction to the High Court to dispose of the
testamentary case at the earliest.
We have perused the consent terms. We do not
find any legal impediment in accepting the same.
The Consent Terms are therefore accepted by us. IA
No.169161 of 2026 is accordingly, allowed.
Taking note of the submissions of learned
counsel for the respective parties as well as the
parties, who have appeared before this Court and
also taking note of the consent terms arrived at
2

--- Page 32 ---
between the parties dated 25.05.2026, we dispose
of this Special Leave Petition with a request to
Bombay  High  Court  to  dispose  of  Testamentary
Petition  No.2444  of  2021  as  expeditiously  as
possible  having  regard  to  the  fact  that  the
parties have arrived at an amicable settlement of
all their disputes concerning the testamentary
case.
The Consent Terms dated 25.05.2026 shall be
read as a part and parcel of this order.

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
Criminal Appeal No.4981 of 2026
[ @ Special Leave Petition (Crl.) No.4788 of 2026 ]
Vijay Pal @ Anku ...Appellant
Versus
State of Himachal Pradesh & Ors.      ...Respondents
O R D E R
Leave granted.
2. The application filed before the High Court for quashing the
proceedings in Sessions Trial No.2084 of 2025 pending before the
Additional  Sessions  Judge,  Fast  Track  Court  (POCSO),  Kangra  at
Dharamshala, District Kangra, Himachal Pradesh was rejected.
3. The appellant had taken a contention before the High Court
that he had married the victim and they have a child born in the
wedlock. We hence directed the parties to be present online, which
was; by order dated 12.10.2026, directed to be facilitated in the
office of the Superintendent of Police, Kangra, Himachal Pradesh.
4. Today when the matter was called, the accused and the victim
were present and so was the mother of the victim. We interacted
with the parties, and we are convinced that the FIR was registered
1

--- Page 41 ---
on a mistaken impression. We are also told that the couple are
married and they have a child and they are living happily.
5. In that circumstance, we are of the opinion that there is no
reason  to  continue  the proceedings in  Sessions Trial  No.2084 of
2025 pending before the Additional Sessions Judge, Fast Track Court
(POCSO),  Kangra  at  Dharamshala,  District  Kangra,  Himachal
Pradesh. We quash the above proceedings and direct the learned
Sessions Judge to cancel the bail bonds, if any, executed by the
appellant.
6. The appeal is allowed.
7. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand rejected.
.......…….……………………. J.
              (J. B. PARDIWALA)
....……….……………………. J.

Ms. Swati Ghildiyal, AOR
                   Ms. Neha Singh, Adv.
                                      
                   Mr. Bharat Bagla, Adv.

                   Mr. Aaditya Aniruddha Pande- 3259, AOR
1

--- Page 49 ---
                   Mr. Shrirang B. Varma, Adv.                   
          Upon hearing the counsel, the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1) Leave granted.
2) The appellants have been convicted for the
offences punishable under Sections 143, 147, 302
read with Sections 149, 376(2)(e) and 376(2)(g) of
the Indian Penal Code, 1860 (for short, ‘the IPC’)
and sentenced them to undergo rigorous imprisonment
for life with fine, in connection with Sessions
Case No. 634 of 2004 arising out of F.I.R. No.59 of
2002, registered at P.S.: Godhra Town, District:
Panchmahal, Gujarat.  The High Court vide impugned
order dated 04.05.2017 confirmed the conviction and
sentence  of  the  Trial  Court.   Aggrieved,  the
appellants are before us. 
3) We have heard the learned Senior Counsel
appearing  for  the  appellants,  the  learned
Additional  Solicitor  General  appearing  for  the
respondent(s) and have also perused the material
placed on record. 
4) The  learned  Senior  Counsel  for  the
appellants submits that the appellants have been
under incarceration for more than seventeen years.
One of the appellants has not been named in the
F.I.R. and has been implicated after a substantial
2

--- Page 50 ---
period of nearly three years after the occurrence. 
5) Incidentally, it is submitted that one of
the appellants is severely handicapped. 
6) The learned A.S.G. for the respondent(s)
vehemently opposed the contentions raised by the
learned Senior Counsel for the appellants, on the
premise that both the Courts below have taken into
consideration  the  relevant  material  brought  on
record, especially when the offence committed is so
serious. 
7) Having heard the parties, suffice it to
state  that  the  appellants  have  been  under
incarceration for a period of more than seventeen
years, and also the fact remains that one of the
appellants has a severe handicap. Certainly, there
are issues to be decided in the appeal, both on
fact and law. 
8) In view of the above and considering the
issues involved in this appeal, we suspend the
sentence and grant bail to the appellants on such
terms and conditions as may be imposed by the
concerned Trial Court.
9) List the appeal for hearing in its due
course. 
 (SHUBHAM SONI)                                  (POONAM VAID)
COURT MASTER (SH)                             ASSISTANT  REGISTRAR
3

--- Page 51 ---

MOHAMMAD FIROZ TOHID KHAN                          Petitioner(s)

THE STATE OF MADHYA PRADESH                        Respondent(s)
(FOR ADMISSION)
(IA No. 287521/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.)
Date : 28-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE J.B. PARDIWALA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE K. VINOD CHANDRAN
For Petitioner(s) :Ms. Saubhagya Sundriyal, Adv.
                   Mr. G. Arudhra Rao, Adv.
                   Mr. Aftab Khan, Adv.
                   Mr. Narveer Yadav, Adv.
                   Mr. Yash Yadav, Adv.
                   Mr. Aniruddha Purushotham- 3414, AOR            
For Respondent(s) : 
                   Mr. Abhimanyu Singh, Adv.
                   Mr. Bittu Kumar Singh, Adv.
                   Mr. Pashupathi Nath Razdan- 2054, AOR           
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The Petitioner was convicted and sentenced to three years
of rigorous imprisonment with fine of Rs. 2000/- by the
Trial Court for an offence under Section 420 of the Indian
Penal Code, 1860, in connection with FIR No. 641 of 2024
registered at P.S. Biaora, Rajgarh, Madhya Pradesh. The
appeal against conviction is pending before the High
Court. 

--- Page 55 ---
2
2. It was alleged that the Petitioner in furtherance of
common  intention  with  the  co-accused  to  cheat  the
complainant,  induced  him  to  pay  the  Petitioner
Rs. 7,50,000/- on the false promise that they would secure
him a job in the Railways on the post of Ticket Collector.
3. The Trial Court convicted the Petitioner relying on the
statements of the complainant and prosecution witnesses,
and the recovery of a forged railway joining letter from
his house; while acquitting the co-accused holding that
there is no credible evidence on record against the
co-accused.
4. Vide the impugned order, the High Court suspended the
sentence of the Petitioner on the condition that he
deposits Rs. 2 lakhs and the balance amount of Rs. 5 lakhs
in three equal instalments within a period of three
months.  Petitioner  challenges  this  monetary  condition
imposed on him for the grant of bail and suspension of
sentence. 
5. Learned Counsel for the Petitioner submitted that the
deposit condition of Rs. 2 lakhs and the balance amount is
excessive and based on the disputed transaction; and that
the Petitioner has been in custody for a period of two
months and has no criminal antecedents.
6. In view of the aforesaid, we set aside the condition
imposed by the High Court while suspending the sentence of
the Petitioner; and thereby suspend the substantive order
of sentence of rigorous imprisonment for three years
imposed on the petitioner and order that the petitioner be
released on bail forthwith, if not required in any other
case, subject to terms and conditions that the Court of
Fourth  Additional  Sessions  Judge,  Biaora,  District
Rajgarh, may deem fit to impose , pending the final dis -
posal of his appeal before the High Court.

--- Page 56 ---
3
7. With the above direction, the Special Leave Petition is
disposed of.
8. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand rejected. 
 (VISHAL ANAND)                                    (POOJA SHARMA)
DEPUTY REGISTRAR                                 COURT MASTER (NSH)

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The State of Maharashtra has filed a status report indicating
that the balance land, possession of which is yet to be handed over
admeasures 4.81 acres. The possession of the balance land is due by
31.12.2026.
2. With respect to the tender process, it is explained that after
the tender document is uploaded, the process of receiving bids will
begin  and  45  days  will  be  granted  for  submission  of  bids.
Thereafter, the technical scrutiny, shortlisting of bidders and
opening of financial bids will take place. The Steering Committee
of the High Court will be approached to obtain approval at each of
the aforesaid stages. Since the tender project cost is around
Rs.4,000/- crores, the approval of the State Government will also
be obtained.
3. On  this  premise,  it  is  stated  that  the  timeline  for
finalisation of tender and selection of the contractor, as recorded
by this Court in Paragraph 4 of the order dated 07.08.2026, may be
extended up to 30.04.2027.  In the same way, time to hand over the
balance 4.81 acres of land may also be extended to 30.04.2027.
4. In the interest of justice and owing to the fact that sincere
efforts are being made to expedite the formulation of the project,
we accede to the request made by the State Government. However, the
next status report shall be filed before 09.12.2026.
2

--- Page 62 ---
5. List on 09.12.2026.

Ms. Mrinal Gopal ELker, AOR
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Exemption Applications are allowed.
2. The petitioner who has been in judicial custody for over one
year and two months since 14.08.2025 in connection with FIR
No. 145 of 2025 registered at Bahrol Police Station, District
Sagar, Madhya Pradesh under Sections 65(1) of Bharatiya Nyaya
Sanhita, 2023 and Sections 5(j)(ii), 5(l) read with 6 Protection
of Children from Sexual Offences Act, 2012 is seeking regular
bail.

--- Page 112 ---
2
3. The Petitioner, aged 23 years is accused of repeatedly raping
the 15-year-old victim leading to her pregnancy, which was later
terminated on the basis of a Court order. Further, the DNA
report of the foetus has matched with that of the Petitioner. 
4. The learned Counsel for the petitioner submitted that the victim
and her parents have turned hostile during the trial wherein
they denied the act of rape and stated that the victim was of
age 20 at the time of the incident; and that the school
admission register relied onto ascertain the victim’s age was
not based on the birth certificate. It was further submitted
that the Petitioner has no criminal antecedents. It is the case
of the Petitioner that the parties were in a relationship and
that the FIR was filed due to the pressure from the villagers as
the Petitioner did not belong to the same caste as that of the
victim. 
5. On a prima facie consideration of the material on record and
after hearing the learned counsel for the parties, we are of the
opinion that the petitioner can be released on bail, on such
terms and conditions that the Court of Ld. Special Judge
(POCSO), Banda, Sagar (the jurisdictional court) may deem fit
and appropriate to impose. Ordered accordingly.
The petitioner shall cooperate with the trial.
6. With  the  above  directions,  the  Special  Leave  Petition  is
disposed of.
7. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed of.
 (VISHAL ANAND)                                    (POOJA SHARMA)

3. Considering the overall facts and circumstances, we are
not inclined to interfere with the impugned judgment/order(s).
4. However, in the event the prosecution evidence has not
been concluded within a period of six months from today, the
petitioners are at liberty to renew the application(s) for
bail before the Trial Court concerned.
5. Needless to state that if such application(s) is/are
filed, the same will be considered independently on its own
merits uninfluenced by any of the observations made in the
order of the High Court or in the present order.
6. The Special Leave Petitions are disposed of in the above
terms.
7. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand disposed
of.
(NIRMALA NEGI)                                  (NIKITA SINGH)

ITEM NO.15               COURT NO.1               SECTION X
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
Writ Petition(s)(Civil) No(s).1286/2026
OBC FEDERATION OF INDIA                            Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
UNION OF INDIA & ORS.                              Respondent(s)
Date : 28-10-2026 This petition was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE THE CHIEF JUSTICE
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE JOYMALYA BAGCHI
         HON'BLE MRS. JUSTICE V. MOHANA
For Petitioner(s) :Mr. Rameshwar Thakur, Adv.
                   Mr. Ashok Sharma, adv.
                   Mrs. Anjani Aiyagari- 1155, AOR
                   Ms. Vandana Khanorkar, Adv.
                                      
For Respondent(s) : 
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The  OBC  Federation  of  India,  claiming  itself  to  be  a
registered society, has approached this Court under Article 32 of
the Constitution of India, seeking a direction that the recognised
list of the Other Backward Castes (OBCs) be pre-loaded as a drop-
down menu in the digital census tools against the caste question
(Column No.10) of the census 2027, the schedule whereof has been
notified on 14.08.2026. It is averred that as per the population
enumeration schedule notified on 14.08.2026, while Column No.10
provides for Scheduled Castes/Scheduled Tribes/Caste, but apart
from SC/ST, there is an open text field and each person has to
describe their caste in their own words. 
2. Learned counsel for the petitioner contends that an open field
1

--- Page 156 ---
will repeat the failure of the Socio-Economic Caste Census 2011,
and that the Union of India/statutory authority should add a drop-
down menu for selection of castes in the same manner as the
practice was adopted in the Socio-Economic Caste Census 2011. 
3. The issue highlighted, in our considered opinion, essentially
pertains to a policy decision, which can be effectively addressed
by the policy makers. 
4. We, therefore, without expressing any opinion on merits,
dispose of this Writ Petition with liberty to the petitioner to
approach the Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India, to
whom  a  copy  of  this  writ  petition  may  be  forwarded  as  a
representation, to enable that authority to look into the issue
highlighted on behalf of the petitioner.
5. Pending applications, if any, also stand disposed of.
(ARJUN BISHT)                              (PREETHI DILEEP KUMAR)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        DY. REGISTRAR
2

--- Page 157 ---
